### ODE 22 [= *Faith Wisdom* <note>original: "Pistis Sophia"</note>, pp. 154—160].

1 He who brought me down from the height and brought me up from the depths. 2 And he who gathers from the middle ᵃ and taught me. 3 He who scattered my enemies and dispersed them. 4 He who gave me authority over bonds so that I might loosen them. 5 He who overthrew by my hands the evil one, the serpent with seven heads, and set me up over his roots that his land might be destroyed. 6 And you were there for them as a destroyer, and you acted so that your name might be a building for me. 7 You destroyed their corruption ᵇ for the evil dominion ᵇ. 8 And I came from you, for you saved ᶜ those who were in your destruction. 9 And you rested them from death, and saved them from the powers. 10 And you went out to the heights of death, and saved them from the powers. 11 And you went out to the heights.

<margin>
ᵃ Read: "taught me." Compare the Coptic: *and taught me*.
ᵇ—ᵇ Coptic: *the poison of him who speaks [evil]*.
ᶜ Coptic: *you liberated*.
</margin>

Ode 22. In verse 6 the Coptic ‘in every place your name surrounded me’ requires, as Schulthess <note>Friedrich Schulthess, a 19th-century scholar of Syriac</note> points out, that we should emend the Syriac word for "building" to "surrounded." So too Diettrich <note>Gustav Diettrich, a scholar of the Odes</note>.

<summary>Ode 21 describes a mystical transformation where the speaker puts on "garments of light," while Ode 22 focuses on Christ's descent into the depths to conquer a seven-headed serpent and liberate captives.</summary>

### ODE 20.

^1 For I am a priest of the Lord, and to Him I minister, and to Him I offer the sacrifice of His thought. ^2 For His thoughts are not like the world, nor like the flesh, nor like those who serve according to the flesh. ^3 But the sacrifice of the Lord is righteousness, and purity of heart and of lips. ^4 Offer your inward being <term>literally "kidneys," representing the seat of emotion and conscience</term> without blemish, and let not your mercies be oppressed by your times, and let not your soul oppress another soul. ^5 You should not acquire a neighbor <insert>by the wounding</insert> of a pure soul. <note>The manuscript contains a scribal error here, repeating "wounding" twice; the translation reflects the intended sense of avoiding harm to others.</note> ^6 And you should not build a structure to destroy your neighbor, nor should you envy the purity of his salvation. ^7 But put on the grace of the Lord without envy, and come into His Paradise, and make for yourself a crown from its tree. ^8 And place it on your head and be glad, and rest upon His rest, and you shall be honored in His glory. ^9 And you shall receive of His sweetness and of His grace, and you shall be radiant in the glory of His holiness. Glory and life to His name. Hallelujah.

<note>a-a The manuscript has "of the wounding, of the wounding," which is clearly corrupt: the word was likely repeated by an eye-error from the previous line, and the correction to "neighbor" or "possession" is a common scholarly emendation.</note>

# ODES OF SOLOMON

4 I see the Lord, and the revelation speaks to me. 5 She <note>The "revelation" or "wisdom" is personified as feminine in Syriac grammar.</note> is skilled in her craftsmanship, and I am made complete through her. 6 I will open my mouth, and the Spirit shall speak through me: 7 The praise of the Lord and His grace; the works of His hands and the labor of His will. 8 For the distinction of His hands, and for the reception of His Word. 9 For the Word of the Lord searches out that which is invisible, and reveals His thought. 10 For the Work is new and living, and the place is clear for His thought. 11 He established the earth, and He set the waters in the sea. 12 He stretched out the heavens, and He fixed the stars; and He established the creation and set it upright. 13 And He rested from His works. 14 And the created things go on in their fullness, and they act according to their courses. 15 And they do not know how to rest <note>Literally "they do not know the soul," implying they lack the self-awareness to cease their appointed tasks.</note> from their Maker; and His powers are the fulfillment of His Word. 16 His thought is a treasury of light, and the night is the abundance of life. 17 For He made the clarity of the day to be light; but the appearance of the darkness is the night for all the days that come. 18 And the fullness of life is given from the One who is the Giver of all. 19 And there is nothing that is outside of the Lord, because He existed before everything came to be. 20 And the worlds happened by His Word, and by the thought of His heart. Praise to Him and honor to His name. Hallelujah.

## ODE 17.

1 I was crowned by my God; my crown is living. 2 I was justified by my Lord; for my salvation is incorruptible. 3 I was released from vanities, and I am not condemned. 4 The choking bonds <note>original: "ܚܢܩ̈ܐ" (hanqe) - refers to strangling knots or suffocating chains.</note> were cut by my hands; I received strength and was helped. New bridles were brought forth, and I hung their crowns upon them. 5 The thought of truth led me, and I walked in it and did not wander.

### ODE 15.

^1^ Just as the sun is a joy to those who seek its light, so the Lord is my light. ^2^ For He is my sun, and His day is without end; before the name of the Lord, all darkness flees away. ^3^ In Him my house has life, and the Lord is the life-giving breath for my soul. ^4^ He has acquired for me hands <note>The Syriac implies "strength" or "possessions" through the metaphor of hands</note> and I minister to them. ^5^ He has become for me a thought of light, and I have been spread out <note>or "extended/expanded"</note> by His hand. ^6^ And the treasury of His grace is beautiful; light has appeared to me, and I have received the faith of salvation from Him, which shall not be abandoned. ^7^ For His love has helped me. ^8^ And after love came majesty <note>original Greek: μεγαλοπρέπεια (megaloprepeia)</note> in abundance. ^8^ <note>Verse numbering repeats in original</note> Night does not arrive instead of the sun's dwelling, and I have stripped off all corruption from myself. ^9^ And immortality <note>original Greek: τὸ θνητόν (to thneton) - the translator notes the Greek term for "mortality" was replaced by "immortality" in the Syriac thought</note> has come in place of that which is of the flesh; and through the Word I have not been defiled. ^10^ And by the hand of the Lord, I have ascended into deathless life <note>original Greek: ζωὴ ἀθάνατος (zoe athanatos)</note>. ^11^ I have been made known to the Giver of Joy; and those who live in themselves are without life. Hallelujah.

### ODE 11.

1 My heart was circumcised <note>A metaphor for spiritual purification or the removal of worldly desires.</note> and its flower appeared, and grace sprang up in it, and it yielded fruit for the Lord. 2 For the Most High circumcised me by His Holy Spirit, and He uncovered my inward being toward Him, and filled me with His love. 3 And His circumcising became my salvation, and I ran in the Way in His peace, in the way of truth. 4 From the beginning even until the end I acquired knowledge. 5 And I was established upon the rock of truth, where the Lord had set me. 6 And speaking waters <note>original: "mayya melle"; likely a reference to the "Living Water" or the Holy Spirit.</note> touched my lips from the fountain of the Lord without grudging. 7 And I drank and was inebriated with the living water that does not die. 8 And my inebriation was not one without knowledge, but I forsook vanity. 9 And I turned toward the Lord my God, and I was enriched by His bounty; and I left the folly that is cast upon the earth, and I stripped it off and cast it from me. 10 And the Lord renewed me in His garment <note>A scholarly note suggests reading "joy" instead of "garment."</note> and possessed me by His light, and from above He gave me rest in incorruption. 11 And I became like the land which blossoms and rejoices in its fruits. 12 And the Lord was like the sun shining upon the face of the land. 13 He lightened my eyes, and my face received the dew; and my breath enjoyed the pleasant odor of the Lord. 14 And He carried me into His Paradise, where is the abundance of the pleasure of the Lord. 15 And I worshipped the Lord on account of His glory; and I said, Blessed, O Lord, are they who are planted in Thy land, and who have a place in Thy Paradise.

<summary>Ode 11 describes a mystical conversion experience using the imagery of spiritual circumcision, drinking living water, and being transported into a divine Paradise. It emphasizes the transition from ignorance and vanity to divine knowledge and incorruption.</summary>

<keywords>Odes of Solomon, spiritual circumcision, living water, Paradise, mystical union, enlightenment, Syriac poetry</keywords>

# ODE 9.

1 Open your ears, and I will speak to you the word of peace; and give me your souls, that I also may give you my soul. 2 The commandments of the Lord and His holy ways, and the holy thought which has been glorified concerning His Messiah <note>original: "Mshikha" (Anointed One). The text emphasizes the divine plan centered on Christ.</note>. 3 For hear me, my beloved ones, and bring your crowns in the eternal life. 4 And receive the new life; let your glories live by His grace. 5 And be strengthened and established in His love. 6 For I proclaim to you the truth concerning Him <margin>For I announce peace to you; compare Isaiah 52:7 <note>original Greek: "εὐαγγελίζω γὰρ ὑμῖν εἰρήνην"</note></margin> for perfection. 7 The perfection of the Lord, let not your souls fall into bitterness. 8 And those who are in His love, let them not be hindered by their own will; let them not fall. 9 Eternal purity is His will. Blessed are those who are commanded by it in their lives, and the narrow ways of life <note>A reference to the difficult path of righteousness, similar to Matthew 7:14.</note>. 10 For the Word makes known to those who exist. 11 And the victory of the Messiah is yours. 12 Purity is in the covenant <margin>Thus in the manuscript <note>original: "Sic cod."</note></margin> of the heavens of the Lord. 13 Perfection, you have peace; the soul of a new repentance. 14 For perfection is the Word, which becomes the Messiah for you. You were first established in purity.

### ODE 7.

1 As the will of the Messiah <note>original: "Mshikha"</note> is toward perfection, so from Him went forth knowledge <note>term: "knowledge" (Syriac: 'ida'ta) - often refers to a spiritual or mystical understanding of God</note> upon the righteous. 2 For His soul rejoices in righteousness, and like the light His soul rejoices. 3 For I have the Lord as my guide <margin>The Syriac text here seems to be an attempt to translate the Greek phrase "For I have the Lord as my helper."</margin>; His soul is without envy <margin>original: "aphthonos" - meaning generous or without grudge.</margin>. 4 In His simplicity, He made Himself known to the Lord in the greatness <insert>of those who spoke</insert>. 5 For He became like me, so that I might find Him; and He appeared like a man, because He put on [humanity]. 7 And His appearance was not as it should be <note>likely referring to the humility of the incarnation</note>, because He became new. 8 How then should it be, so that they might be saved? And His preparation comes so that the whole might not be lost. 9 Knowledge went forth in the Divinity that He is. 10 She <note>Knowledge/Wisdom is often feminine in Syriac</note> comforts the darkness; she is beloved above all things. 11 And He told me His lack of knowledge; let us know who it was that made light in Him. 12 Because of this, He rejoices in life and grace, and as one who is known by Him, and according to His sacrifice <margin>The scholar Nestle suggests reading "essence" (ousia) instead of "sacrifice" (thysia) here.</margin>. 13 Because He became knowledge without corruption <margin>original: "aphthartos" - see also Romans 1:23</margin>, the fullness of the worlds and their Father. 14 He allowed Himself to be seen by His own <margin>original: "tois idiois autou" - a reference to John 1:11, "He came to His own."</margin> so that they might know Him. 15 Because He promised to reveal them, so that they should not fall, those who were from the beginning. 16 For He rejoices in knowledge, and in it He wondered.

---

<note>a—a: without hindrance (Greek: akolytos).</note>
<note>b—b: Apparently an attempt to translate "For I have the Lord as my helper."</note>

<note>d: Thus in the manuscript.</note>
<note>e: sacrifice (Greek: thysia), for which Nestle proposes to read "essence" (Greek: ousia).</note>

# ODES OF SOLOMON
->a<-
<meta>Digitized by Google</meta>

...the Spirit went forth. $^8$The world seized it <note>The translator suggests an emendation here; see notes below.</note> and cast it down, and it came to the Temple. $^9$And the world was not able to restrain it, neither the possessions of the sons of men, nor even the enclosures of the waters of the whole world. $^{10}$It came upon the dry dust where they dwelt, and it filled their world. And it gave drink to the dryness of the dwellers <margin>Read: "upon the thirsty land" (based on Isaiah 35:1) and compare the Coptic below.</margin>. $^{11}$And it gladdened their souls, even those who were far from the thirst that is removed from it. $^{12}$Blessed are the servants of that Cup-bearer, those who have been entrusted with His life. $^{13}$They received joy of heart, and they began to be loosed who had been bound. $^{14}$And that light went forth to all of them, even those who were in light. $^{15}$And those who were high were restored and established <margin>Double translation of the Greek word for "set upright" (compare Hebrews 12:12).</margin>. $^{16}$And they received strength <margin>Greek: "they were strong" (Isaiah 35:3).</margin> for their coming and for their eyes.

***

**Ode 6, v. 8.** I should suggest an <term>emendation</term> <note>A correction made to a text where the original is suspected to be corrupted.</note> to the Syriac, "led" <note>original: ܕܒܪ (dbar)</note> for "seized" <note>original: ܕܠܦܛ (dlaphat)</note>, but it is not borne out by the Coptic, which has in the <term>Targum</term> <note>An interpretive translation or paraphrase.</note> ‘and led them over the temple,’ and in the text ‘and it turned toward the temple.’ ‘Led’ would answer to the Syriac "led," but an object to the verb is wanting. Probably the missing word is "them."

**v. 9.** The Coptic text suggests that a line has dropped in the Syriac: the comment has ‘they were not able to contain it [the closed places] nor the built places,’ and the text has ‘they were not able to contain him in fortified and built places.’

**v. 10.** There seems to be a slight displacement in the Syriac; for the Coptic Targum has ‘those living in the dry sand drank,’ and the text has ‘those who inhabited the dry sand drank.’

**v. 13.** The Coptic shows some variation: the comment has ‘those who are loosed received vigor in me,’ and the text has ‘those who were loosed were receiving joy of heart.’

**v. 16.** The Syriac has <term>parousia</term> <note>original: παρουσία - presence or arrival</note> where the Coptic has <term>parrhesia</term> <note>original: παρρησία - boldness or plainness of speech</note>. Neither Greek word makes very easy reading. Perhaps the Greek was "for their paralysis" <note>original: παρέσει αὐτῶν or παραλύσει αὐτῶν</note>, in which case we translate ‘they received strength for their paralysed state and light for their [darkened] eyes.’ Compare Isaiah 35:3, 5.

<summary>This page provides a translation and critical commentary on Ode 6 of the Odes of Solomon, comparing Syriac and Coptic variants to resolve linguistic difficulties. The text focuses on the life-giving "Spirit" or "Water" that restores the thirsty and strengthens the weak.</summary>

<keywords>Ode 6, Syriac, Coptic, textual criticism, Temple, Isaiah 35, healing, parousia, parrhesia, emendation</keywords>

# ODE 4.

<sup>1</sup> No man, <note>original: "shachlep"</note> O Lord my God, can change Your holy place, <sup>a</sup> <margin>Perhaps: "change immediately"</margin> <sup>2</sup> nor is there anyone who can change it and place it in another location, because he has no authority over it. <sup>3</sup> For You possessed Your place before You created any other places. <sup>4</sup> And that which is established shall not be changed from that time when it was in Your hands. <sup>5</sup> For who, O Lord, is able to change Your designs? And when You return from destruction, <sup>13</sup> and because You are <sup>d</sup> unchangeable and do not vary <sup>d</sup> <margin>Probably a double translation (perhaps from the Greek: "hyposteiles")</margin>. <sup>14</sup> Concerning that which You have chosen, O my God, it shall be for us.
